Appendix A
Appendix

With the ansatz eq.(
24
) the following relations hold:

\displaystyle\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}

\displaystyle=\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&-\boldsymbol{\kappa}-P_{11}^{\prime}(x_{2})\\
0&0&0\\
P_{33}^{\prime}(x_{2})&0&0\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\hskip 17.00024pt\mbox{tr}\left(\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}\right)=\boldsymbol{0}\,,

\displaystyle\mbox{skew}\,\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&-\boldsymbol{\kappa}-P_{11}^{\prime}(x_{2})-P_{33}^{\prime}(x_{2})\\
0&0&0\\
\boldsymbol{\kappa}+P_{11}^{\prime}(x_{2})+P_{33}^{\prime}(x_{2})&0&0\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\,\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}

\displaystyle=\mbox{sym}\,\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&P_{33}^{\prime}(x_{2})-\boldsymbol{\kappa}-P_{11}^{\prime}(x_{2})\\
0&0&0\\
P_{33}^{\prime}(x_{2})-\boldsymbol{\kappa}-P_{11}^{\prime}(x_{2})&0&0\\
\end{array}\right)\,.

Given eq.(
A
) we observe

\begin{array}[]{l}\left\lVert\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}\right\rVert^{2}\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\left\lVert\mbox{sym}\,\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}\right\rVert^{2}=\left\lVert\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\,\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}\right\rVert^{2}\end{array}

(161)

and also

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pta_{1}\left\lVert\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\,\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}\right\rVert^{2}+a_{2}\left\lVert\mbox{skew}\,\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}\right\rVert^{2}+\frac{a_{3}}{3}\,\mbox{tr}^{2}\left(\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt=\frac{1}{2}\left(a_{1}\left(\boldsymbol{\kappa}+P_{11}^{\prime}(x_{2})-P_{33}^{\prime}(x_{2})\right)^{2}+a_{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\kappa}+P_{11}^{\prime}(x_{2})+P_{33}^{\prime}(x_{2})\right)^{2}\right)

(162)

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt=\frac{1}{2}\left((a_{1}+a_{2})\left(\left(P_{11}^{\prime}(x_{2})+\boldsymbol{\kappa}\right)^{2}+P_{33}^{\prime}(x_{2})^{2}\right)-2(a_{1}-a_{2})P_{33}^{\prime}(x_{2})\left(\boldsymbol{\kappa}+P_{11}^{\prime}(x_{2})\right)\right)\,.

Appendix B
Generalized Neumann boundary conditions for the relaxed micromorphic model and the Cosserat model

Partial integration for the matrix-Curl operator can be written as

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\left\langle\boldsymbol{P}\times\boldsymbol{\nu},\boldsymbol{Q}\right\rangle\,\mbox{dS}

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int\limits_{\Omega}\left\langle\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{P},\boldsymbol{Q}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{P},\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{Q}\right\rangle\mbox{d}x\,,

(163)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\left\langle\boldsymbol{P}\times\boldsymbol{\nu},\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right\rangle\,\mbox{dS}

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int\limits_{\Omega}\left\langle\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{P},\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{P},\mbox{Curl}\,\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right\rangle\mbox{d}x\,,

where
\boldsymbol{P,Q}\in\mbox{C}^{1}\left(\overline{\Omega},\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}\right)
are sufficiently smooth square
3\times 3
matrix fields and
\nu
is the outward unit normal vector to
\partial\Omega
.
Inserting
\boldsymbol{P}=\delta\boldsymbol{A}
and
\boldsymbol{Q}=\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{A}
for
\boldsymbol{A}\in\mbox{C}^{1}\left(\overline{\Omega},\mathfrak{so}(3)\right)
, for a test field
\delta\boldsymbol{A}
and argument
\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathfrak{so}(3)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{A}\times\boldsymbol{\nu},\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right\rangle\,\mbox{dS}=\displaystyle\int\limits_{\Omega}\left\langle\mbox{Curl}\,\delta\boldsymbol{A},\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right\rangle-\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{A},\mbox{Curl}\,\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right\rangle\mbox{d}x\,.

(164)

The scalar-product on the left-hand side is interpreted row-wise.
Making use of the permutation properties of the scalar product, namely

\langle\boldsymbol{a}\times\boldsymbol{\nu},\boldsymbol{b}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}=\mbox{det}\left[\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{\nu}\right]=-\mbox{det}\left[\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b},\boldsymbol{\nu}\right]=-\langle\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\times\boldsymbol{\nu}\rangle

(165)

we arrive at

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{A},\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\times\boldsymbol{\nu}\right\rangle\,\mbox{dS}=\displaystyle\int\limits_{\Omega}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{A},\mbox{Curl}\,\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right\rangle-\left\langle\mbox{Curl}\,\delta\boldsymbol{A},\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right\rangle\mbox{d}x\,.

(166)

Since
\delta\boldsymbol{A}\in\mbox{C}^{1}\left(\overline{\Omega},\mathfrak{so}(3)\right)
this gives equivalently

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{A},\mbox{skew}\left(\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\times\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\right\rangle\,\mbox{dS}=\displaystyle\int\limits_{\Omega}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{A},\mbox{skew}\left(\mbox{Curl}\,\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right)\right\rangle-\left\langle\mbox{Curl}\,\delta\boldsymbol{A},\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right\rangle\mbox{d}x\,.

(167)

Replacing
\boldsymbol{Q}=\boldsymbol{m}=\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}
yields

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{A},\mbox{skew}\left(\boldsymbol{m}\times\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\right\rangle\,\mbox{dS}=\displaystyle\int\limits_{\Omega}\left\langle\delta\boldsymbol{A},\mbox{skew}\left(\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{m}\right)\right\rangle-\left\langle\mbox{Curl}\,\delta\boldsymbol{A},\boldsymbol{m}\right\rangle\mbox{d}x\,,

(168)

where the appropriate localization shows
\mbox{skew}\left(\boldsymbol{m}\times\boldsymbol{\nu}\right)\Bigr|_{\Gamma}=0
since
\delta\boldsymbol{A}
is arbitrary on
\Gamma
.

Appendix C
The Lie-algebra
\mathfrak{so}(3)
, the 3D-Curl on
\mathfrak{so}(3)
and Nye’s relation

Given
\boldsymbol{A}\in\mathfrak{so}(3)

\boldsymbol{A}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&-a_{3}&a_{2}\\
a_{3}&0&-a_{1}\\
-a_{2}&a_{1}&0\end{array}\right)

(169)

the operator axl:
\mathfrak{so}(3)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
is introduced

\mbox{axl}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&-a_{3}&a_{2}\\
a_{3}&0&-a_{1}\\
-a_{2}&a_{1}&0\end{array}\right):=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}a_{1}\\
a_{2}\\
a_{3}\end{array}\right),\hskip 17.00024pt\boldsymbol{A}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}=\left(\mbox{axl}\boldsymbol{A}\right)\times\boldsymbol{v},\hskip 8.50012pt\forall\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,.

(170)

Given the definition eqs. (
169
)-(
170
), the following identities hold (
Nye’s relation
)

-\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{A}=\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\mbox{axl}\boldsymbol{A}\right)^{T}-\mbox{tr}\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\mbox{axl}\boldsymbol{A}\right)^{T}\right]\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\hskip 8.50012pt\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\mbox{axl}\boldsymbol{A}=-\left(\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{A}\right)^{T}+\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\left[\left(\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{A}\right)^{T}\right]\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\,.

(171)

If we now have
\boldsymbol{A}=\mbox{skew}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}
it is possible to show that

-\left(\mbox{Curl}\,\mbox{skew}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)^{T}=\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\,\mbox{axl}\left(\mbox{skew}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right),\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\frac{1}{2}\,\mbox{curl}\boldsymbol{u}=\mbox{axl}\left(\mbox{skew}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right),

(172)

which leads to the following identity for the full Curl,

\left\lVert\mbox{Curl}\,\mbox{skew}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right\rVert^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\mbox{curl}\,\boldsymbol{u}\right\rVert^{2}.

It is also highlighted here that, thanks to eq.(
172
)
1
this relation holds

\mbox{Curl}\,\mbox{skew}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}=-\mbox{Curl}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}

(see
[
15
]
),
since

0=\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}=\mbox{Curl}\,\mbox{skew}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\mbox{Curl}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}

, which implies that choosing the symmetric or the skew-symmetric part of the gradient of the displacement does not make any difference besides a sign.
Moreover
\mbox{tr}(\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{S})=0
for any symmetric matrix
\boldsymbol{S}\in\mbox{Sym}(3)

Appendix D
Cylindrical bending for the isotropic Cosserat continuum with classical notation

In
[
36
]
(eq.(2.2)) there is the correspondence between the isotropic Cosserat model with rotation vector and the Curl representation in dislocation format.
Both have three curvature parameters and the identification is given by

\alpha=\frac{1}{3}\left(4a_{3}-a_{1}\right),\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\beta=\frac{a_{1}-a_{2}}{2},\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\gamma=\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}}{2}\,.

(173)

Setting
\vartheta:=\mbox{axl}(\boldsymbol{A})
and taking into account eqs. (
170
)–(
173
), the expression of the strain energy for the isotropic Cosserat continuum can be equivalently written as:

\displaystyle W\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u},\boldsymbol{A},\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right)=

\displaystyle\,\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\left\lVert\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right\rVert^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}}{2}\mbox{tr}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\mu_{c}\left\lVert\mbox{skew}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}-\boldsymbol{A}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\underbrace{\left(a_{1}\,\left\lVert\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\,\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right\rVert^{2}\,+a_{2}\,\left\lVert\mbox{skew}\,\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right\rVert^{2}\,+\frac{a_{3}}{3}\,\mbox{tr}^{2}\left(\mbox{Curl}\,\boldsymbol{A}\right)\right)}_{\mbox{dislocation tensor format}}

(174)

\displaystyle=W\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u},\boldsymbol{\vartheta},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\vartheta}\right)=

\displaystyle\,\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\left\lVert\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right\rVert^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}}{2}\mbox{tr}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{\mu_{c}}{2}\left\lVert\mbox{curl}\boldsymbol{u}-2\boldsymbol{\vartheta}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\underbrace{\left(\alpha\,\mbox{tr}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\vartheta}\right)+\beta\,\langle\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\vartheta}^{T},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\vartheta}\rangle+\gamma\,\left\lVert\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\vartheta}\right\rVert^{2}\right)}_{\mbox{micro-rotation vector format}}\,,

since

\begin{split}\left\lVert\mbox{skew}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}-\boldsymbol{A}\right\rVert^{2}=2\left\lVert\mbox{axl}(\mbox{skew}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}-\mbox{Anti}(\boldsymbol{\vartheta}))\right\rVert^{2}=2\lVert\frac{1}{2}\mbox{curl}\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{\vartheta}\rVert^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert\mbox{curl}\boldsymbol{u}-2\boldsymbol{\vartheta}\right\rVert^{2}\,.\end{split}

(175)

The equilibrium equations without body forces in the classical notation are now the following

\displaystyle\mbox{Div}\left[2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right]-\mu_{c}\,\mbox{curl}\left[\mbox{curl}\,\boldsymbol{u}-2\boldsymbol{\vartheta}\right]\,

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{0}\,,

(176)

\displaystyle\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\mbox{Div}\left[\alpha\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\vartheta}\right)\,\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}+\beta\,\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\vartheta}\right)^{T}+\gamma\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\vartheta}\,\right]+2\mu_{c}\,\left(\mbox{curl}\,\boldsymbol{u}-2\boldsymbol{\vartheta}\right)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{0}\,.

The boundary conditions at the upper and lower surface (free surface) are

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\widetilde{t}}(x_{2}=\pm\,h/2)=\pm\,\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\sigma}}(x_{2})\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{2}=\boldsymbol{0}\,,\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\overline{\boldsymbol{\eta}}(x_{2}=\pm\,h/2)=\pm\,\overline{\boldsymbol{m}}(x_{2})\cdot\boldsymbol{e}_{2}=\boldsymbol{0}\,,

(177)

where
\boldsymbol{\widetilde{\sigma}}=2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}+2\mu_{c}\,\left(\mbox{skew}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}-\mbox{Anti}(\boldsymbol{\vartheta})\right)
,
\boldsymbol{e}_{2}
is the unit vector aligned to the
x_{2}
-direction, and the second-order moment stress tensor
\overline{\boldsymbol{m}}:=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\left(\alpha\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\vartheta}\right)\,\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}+\beta\,\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\vartheta}\right)^{T}+\gamma\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\vartheta}\right)
.
The relation to the higher-order stress tensor reported in Sect.
6
is the following:

\mbox{dev}\left(\boldsymbol{m}^{T}\right)=-\mbox{dev}\left(\overline{\boldsymbol{m}}\right)\,,\hskip 17.00024pt\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{m}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\left(\overline{\boldsymbol{m}}\right)\,.

(178)

According to the reference system shown in Fig.
1
, the ansatz for the displacement field and the micro-rotation vector is

\boldsymbol{u}(x_{1},x_{2})=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{1}\,x_{1}x_{2}\\
v(x_{2})+\frac{\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{1}x_{1}^{2}}{2}\\
0\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\boldsymbol{\vartheta}(x_{1},x_{2})=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0\\
0\\
-\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{2}\,x_{1}\\
\end{array}\right)\,.

(179)

Substituting the ansatz eq.(
179
) in eq.(
176
) the equilibrium equations result in

\begin{split}2\mu_{c}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{2})-\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{1}\lambda_{e}+\left(\lambda_{e}+2\mu_{e}\right)v^{\prime\prime}(x_{2})=0\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\,\,2x_{1}\mu_{c}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{1})=0\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\,\,2x_{1}\mu_{c}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\kappa}_{2})=0\,,\end{split}

(180)

which are exactly the same as the eq.(
91
) in Section
6
. Since also the boundary conditions eq.(
177
) are equivalent to the boundary condition eq.(
88
) in Section
6
, further calculations are avoided.
It is nevertheless interesting to show the definition of the higher-order bending moment:

M_{\mbox{m}}(\boldsymbol{\kappa})=\displaystyle\int\limits_{-h/2}^{h/2}\langle\overline{\boldsymbol{m}}\,\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\rangle\,\mbox{d}x_{2}=\displaystyle\int\limits_{-h/2}^{h/2}\langle\left(\boldsymbol{m}\times\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\right)\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\rangle\,\mbox{d}x_{2}=h\,\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\gamma\,\boldsymbol{\kappa}\,,

(181)

given that

\langle\left(\boldsymbol{m}\times\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\right)\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\rangle=\langle\left(\boldsymbol{m},\boldsymbol{e}_{2}\times\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\right),\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\rangle=-\langle\boldsymbol{m}\,\boldsymbol{e}_{3},\boldsymbol{e}_{1}\rangle=-\langle\boldsymbol{m}^{T}\,\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\rangle=-\langle\overline{\boldsymbol{m}}\,\boldsymbol{e}_{1},\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\rangle\,.

(182)

D.1
Lakes formula

In order to connect ourselves to the existing literature, we provide the reader with an excerpt taken from Lakes
[
22
]
to which we compare our results:
“Cosserat solids may be characterized via size effects in rigidity. Exact analytical solutions
for size effects form the basis of a variety of experiments for the characterization of Cosserat solids.
For example, Gauthier and Jahsman
[
14
]
give
\Omega
,
the ratio of rigidity to its classical value
, for cylindrical bending of a plate

\Omega:=\left(1+24\frac{\ell_{b}^{2}\,\left(1-\nu\right)}{h^{2}}\right),\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\ell_{b}=\sqrt{\frac{\gamma}{4\mu}}

(183)

with
h
the plate thickness”.

In plate bending, the anticlastic curvature due to the Poisson’s effect is constrained, in contrast to (classical) beam bending.

\displaystyle M^{\mbox{\small Lakes}}_{\mbox{tot}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{E}\,J_{x_{3}}}{R}\left(1+24\left(\frac{l_{b}}{h}\right)^{2}\left(1-\nu\right)\right)=\frac{E}{1-\nu^{2}}\frac{h^{3}}{12}\frac{1}{R}\left(1+24\frac{\gamma}{4\mu}\frac{1}{h^{2}}\left(1-\nu\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{E}{1-\nu^{2}}\frac{h^{3}}{12}\frac{1}{R}\left(1+24\gamma\,\frac{(1+\nu)}{2\,E}\frac{1}{h^{2}}\left(1-\nu\right)\right)=\frac{E}{1-\nu^{2}}\frac{h^{3}}{12}\frac{1}{R}+\frac{\gamma\,h}{R}\,,

\displaystyle M_{\mbox{tot}}=

\displaystyle\frac{4\mu_{e}\left(\lambda_{e}+\mu_{e}\right)}{\lambda_{e}+2\mu_{e}}\,\frac{h^{3}}{12}\,\boldsymbol{\kappa}+h\,\widetilde{\gamma}\,\boldsymbol{\kappa}=\frac{E}{1-\nu^{2}}\,\frac{h^{3}}{12}\,\frac{1}{R}+\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}\,h}{R}\,,

(184)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\kappa}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{R}\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\gamma}:=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\gamma\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\,\,\,\,\,\widehat{E}=\frac{E}{1-\nu^{2}}\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\,\,\,\,\,J_{x_{3}}=\frac{h^{3}}{12}\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\,\,\,\,\,h=2a\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\,\,\,\,\,\mu=\frac{E}{2(1+\nu)}\,,

where
\gamma=\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}}{2}
.
It is possible to define a dimensionless bending moment by dividing by the classical Cauchy bending moment eq.(
13
)
1
obtaining
[
14
,
24
]

\Omega:=\frac{M_{\mbox{tot}}}{M_{\mbox{c}}}=1+24\frac{\gamma}{4\mu}\frac{1}{h^{2}}\left(1-\nu\right)=1+24\left(\frac{\ell_{b}}{h}\right)^{2}\,\left(1-\nu\right)\,,

(185)

which coincide with eq.(
183
).

Appendix E
Equilibrium equation and boundary conditions for the full micromorphic and micro-strain model

The only critical part in this calculus is connected to the used isotropic curvature expression

\begin{split}W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\,\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left(a_{1}\,\left\lVert\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\Big(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\Big)\right\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+a_{2}\,\left\lVert\mbox{skew}\Big(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\Big)\right\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}^{2}\Big(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\Big)\right)\,\mbox{d}x\,.\end{split}

(186)

The first variation of
W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}
with respect to
\boldsymbol{P}
is

\displaystyle\delta W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}=

\displaystyle\,\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(a_{1}\,\langle\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\delta\boldsymbol{P}\right)\rangle+a_{2}\,\langle\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\delta\boldsymbol{P}\right)\rangle

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\delta\boldsymbol{P}\right)\right)\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(a_{1}\,\langle\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\partial_{x_{i}}\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle+a_{2}\,\langle\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\partial_{x_{i}}\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle

(187)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\langle\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\partial_{x_{i}}\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle\right)\,\mbox{d}x\,.

The product rule implies that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{x_{i}}\,\bigg(a_{1}\,\langle\mbox{dev}\,

\displaystyle\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+a_{2}\,\langle\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\langle\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\right)\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(a_{1}\,\langle\partial_{x_{i}}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+a_{2}\,\langle\partial_{x_{i}}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}

(188)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\langle\partial_{x_{i}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\right)\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(a_{1}\,\langle\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\partial_{x_{i}}\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle+a_{2}\,\langle\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\partial_{x_{i}}\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\langle\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\partial_{x_{i}}\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle\right)\,\mbox{d}x\,,

thus
\delta W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}
can be written as

\displaystyle\delta W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}=

\displaystyle\,\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{x_{i}}\,\bigg(a_{1}\,\langle\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+a_{2}\,\langle\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\langle\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\right)\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle-\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(a_{1}\,\langle\partial_{x_{i}}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+a_{2}\,\langle\partial_{x_{i}}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right),\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}

(189)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\langle\partial_{x_{i}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\right)\,\mbox{d}x\,,

or, defining
\boldsymbol{m}_{i}=\mu\,L_{c}\,\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)

+

a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)

+

\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)
,
i=1,2,3
, we can equivalently write

\displaystyle\delta W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}=

\displaystyle\,\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{x_{i}}\,\langle\boldsymbol{m}_{i},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}x-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\partial_{x_{i}}\,\boldsymbol{m}_{i},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}x

(190)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mbox{Div}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\langle\boldsymbol{m}_{1},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\\
\langle\boldsymbol{m}_{2},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\\
\langle\boldsymbol{m}_{3},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\end{array}\right)\,\mbox{d}x-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\partial_{x_{i}}\,\boldsymbol{m}_{i},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}x\,.

After applying the divergence theorem to the first integral of eq.(
189
) and eq.(
190
) respectively, we obtain

\displaystyle\delta W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}=

\displaystyle\,\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\left(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{P}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right)\,n_{i},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}S

\displaystyle-\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{P}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{P}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{P}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\langle\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\boldsymbol{m}_{i}\,n_{i},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\,\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\mbox{d}S-\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\langle a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\Delta P}+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\,\boldsymbol{\Delta P}

(194)

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\Delta P}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\,,\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}x\,,

or equivalently in the form of eq.(
190
)

\displaystyle\delta W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\langle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\langle\boldsymbol{m}_{1},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\\
\langle\boldsymbol{m}_{2},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\\
\langle\boldsymbol{m}_{3},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\end{array}\right),\boldsymbol{n}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}S-\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\boldsymbol{\Delta}\boldsymbol{P}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\boldsymbol{\Delta}\boldsymbol{P}\right)\hskip 31.2982pt

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\Delta}\boldsymbol{P}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\delta\boldsymbol{P}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}x\,.

Since
\delta\boldsymbol{P}
is arbitrary in
\Omega
, and on
\partial\Omega
, and since
\delta W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}=0
for a stationary point, we are now in a position to write the curvature terms of the equilibrium equation and the boundary condition without body forces and external load for the micromorphic model

\displaystyle\mbox{in}\,\Omega

\displaystyle-\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\left[a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\,\underset{\in\,\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}{\boldsymbol{\Delta P}}+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\,\underset{\in\,\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}{\boldsymbol{\Delta P}}+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\underset{\in\,\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}{\boldsymbol{\Delta P}}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right]

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{0}_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,,

(198)

\displaystyle\mbox{on}\,\partial\Omega

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\boldsymbol{m}_{i}\,n_{i}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{0}_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,,

If
\boldsymbol{P}=\boldsymbol{S}
is symmetric, then
\delta\boldsymbol{S}\in\mbox{Sym}(3)
as well and, like in the micro-strain model, eq.(
198
) turns into

\displaystyle\mbox{on}\,\Omega

\displaystyle-\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\left[a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\,\underset{\in\,\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}{\boldsymbol{\Delta S}}+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\underset{\in\,\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}{\boldsymbol{\Delta S}}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right]

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{0}_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,,

(199)

\displaystyle\mbox{on}\,\partial\Omega

\displaystyle\mbox{sym}\left(\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\boldsymbol{m}_{i}\,n_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{0}_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,.

Appendix F
Equilibrium equation for the second gradient elastic model

\displaystyle W=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\left\lVert\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+\frac{\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}}{2}\mbox{tr}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)

(200)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left(a_{1}\,\left\lVert\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\Big(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\Big)\right\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+a_{2}\,\left\lVert\mbox{skew}\Big(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\Big)\right\rVert^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}^{2}\Big(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\Big)\right)\mbox{d}x\,.

It is clear that the proposed curvature energy is isotropic
[
30
]
.
However, the expression is not the most general isotropic curvature term, which would have 5 independent constants (see also
[
29
,
10
]
and Appendix
G
).
The first variation with respect to
\boldsymbol{u}
is

\displaystyle\delta W=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\langle\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u},\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\right)\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(a_{1}\,\langle\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right),\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\right)\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}

\displaystyle+a_{2}\,\langle\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right),\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\right)\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\right)\bigg)\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\langle\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\langle\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle\,\mbox{d}x

(201)

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(a_{1}\,\langle\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right),\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+a_{2}\,\langle\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right),\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\langle\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\partial_{x_{i}}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\bigg)\,\mbox{d}x\,.

The product rule implies that (here reported just for the curvature part of the energy as an example)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{i}}\,\bigg(a_{1}\,\langle\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right),\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+a_{2}\,\langle\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right),\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\langle\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\right)\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(a_{1}\,\langle\partial_{x_{i}}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right),\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+a_{2}\,\langle\partial_{x_{i}}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right),\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}

(202)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\langle\partial_{x_{i}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\right)\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(a_{1}\,\langle\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right),\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}+a_{2}\,\langle\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right),\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\langle\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\right)\,\mbox{d}x\,.

Since

\mbox{div}\left(\boldsymbol{S}^{T}_{1}\,\delta\boldsymbol{u}\right)=\langle\mbox{Div}\,\boldsymbol{S}_{1},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}+\langle\boldsymbol{S}_{1},\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}
,
we can express
\delta W
as

\displaystyle\delta W=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mbox{div}\bigg[\Big(2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)^{T}\,\delta\boldsymbol{u}\bigg]\,\mbox{d}x-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\langle\mbox{Div}\left[2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right],\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{x_{i}}\bigg(\langle a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\bigg)\,\mbox{d}x

(203)

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(\langle a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\bigg)\,\mbox{d}x\,,

or equivalently

\displaystyle\delta W=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mbox{div}\bigg[\Big(2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)\,\delta\boldsymbol{u}\bigg]\,\mbox{d}x-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\langle\mbox{Div}\left[2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right],\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mbox{div}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\langle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{m}}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\\
\langle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{m}}_{2},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\\
\langle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{m}}_{3},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\end{array}\right)\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(\langle a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\bigg)\,\mbox{d}x\,,

where
m_{i}=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(\langle a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\bigg)
.
After applying the divergence theorem to the first and the third term of eq.(
203
) we obtain

\displaystyle\delta W=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\langle\Big(2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)^{T}\delta\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{n}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}S-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\langle\mbox{Div}\left[2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right],\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big),\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\,\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,n_{i}\,\mbox{d}S

(207)

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}x\,.

or equivalently

\displaystyle\delta W=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\langle\Big(2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)\delta\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{n}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}S-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\langle\mbox{Div}\left[2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right],\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\left(\langle\begin{array}[]{c}\langle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{m}}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\\
\langle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{m}}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\\
\langle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{m}}_{1},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\end{array}\right),\boldsymbol{n}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}S

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}x\,.

Integrating by part the fourth term of eq.(
207
) it is possible to write

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mbox{div}\left[\Big(\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)^{T}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\right]\,\mbox{d}x

(211)

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\langle\mbox{Div}\left[\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right)\right],\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}x

and using the divergence theorem on the first term of eq.(
211
) we have

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mbox{div}\left[\Big(\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)^{T}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\right]\,\mbox{d}x

(212)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\left(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right)\,\boldsymbol{n},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}S

Now, the only term that needs further attention is the third one in eq.(
207
), since only the normal component to the surface of
\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}
is independent with respect to
\delta\boldsymbol{u}
.
We introduce these three operators

\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}}=\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}-\boldsymbol{n}\otimes\boldsymbol{n}\right)\cdot(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}},\partial_{x_{3}})\,,\hskip 17.00024pt\boldsymbol{D^{n}_{a}}=\left(\boldsymbol{n}\otimes\boldsymbol{n}\right)\cdot(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}},\partial_{x_{3}})\,,\hskip 17.00024ptD^{n}_{b}=(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}},\partial_{x_{3}})\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\,,

(213)

which, using the fact that
\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\delta u}\right)^{T}=(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}},\partial_{x_{3}})\otimes\boldsymbol{\delta u}
and that
\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}}+\boldsymbol{D^{n}_{a}}=(\partial_{x_{1}},\partial_{x_{2}},\partial_{x_{3}})
, allows us to write the third term in eq.(
207
) as

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)n_{i},\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\,\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}S

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)n_{i},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\otimes\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}S

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)n_{i},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\otimes\boldsymbol{D^{n}_{a}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}S

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt=

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\overbrace{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)n_{i}}^{\textstyle B_{ij}\mathstrut},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\otimes\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}S

(214)

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\left(\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)n_{i}\right)\boldsymbol{n},D^{n}_{b}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}S\,.

While the term involving the normal derivative of the virtual displacement (
D^{n}_{b}\boldsymbol{\partial u}=\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}
) is independent of
\boldsymbol{\delta u}
, the term involving the tangential projection of
\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\delta u}
(
\boldsymbol{\delta u}\otimes\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}}=\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}\delta u}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}-\boldsymbol{n}\otimes\boldsymbol{n}\right)
) is not, and must be further manipulated.
It is known (
[
28
,
29
]
) that this tangential term can be still manipulated integrating by parts and using the surface divergence theorem so implying (see eq.(3.5) in
[
28
]
)

\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\langle\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{\delta u}\otimes\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}}\rangle=-\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\langle\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\left[\boldsymbol{B}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}-\boldsymbol{n}\otimes\boldsymbol{n}\right)\right]\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}-\boldsymbol{n}\otimes\boldsymbol{n}\right),\boldsymbol{\delta u}\rangle+\int\limits_{\partial\partial\Omega}\llbracket\langle\boldsymbol{B}\nu,\boldsymbol{\delta u}\rangle\rrbracket\,,

(215)

where
\nu
is the normal to
\partial\partial\Omega
.
In our bending problem there is no
\partial\partial\Omega
and the normal is constant, so that the preceding equation reduce to

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\langle\boldsymbol{B},\boldsymbol{\delta u}\otimes\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\langle\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\boldsymbol{B}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}-\boldsymbol{n}\otimes\boldsymbol{n}\right)\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}-\boldsymbol{n}\otimes\boldsymbol{n}\right),\boldsymbol{\delta u}\rangle

(216)

\displaystyle=-\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\langle\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}}\boldsymbol{B}\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}-\boldsymbol{n}\otimes\boldsymbol{n}\right),\boldsymbol{\delta u}\rangle=-\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\langle\boldsymbol{B}\cdot\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}},\boldsymbol{\delta u}\rangle\,.

Hence, in our particular case, the first term in eq.(
214
) can be written as:

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)n_{i},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\otimes\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}}\,\mbox{d}S

(217)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\Big[\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)n_{i}\Big]\cdot\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}S\,.

It is now possible to write all together

\displaystyle\delta W=

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\langle\mbox{Div}\left[2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right],\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\langle\mbox{Div}\left[\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right)\right],\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\langle\Big(2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)\,\boldsymbol{n},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}S

(218)

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\left(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right)\,\boldsymbol{n},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}S

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\Big[\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)n_{i}\Big]\cdot\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}S

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\langle\left(\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)n_{i}\right)\boldsymbol{n},D^{n}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}S\,,

or in an equivalent way

\displaystyle\delta W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}=

\displaystyle-\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\langle\mbox{Div}\left(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\right),\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}x+\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\langle\mbox{Div}\left[\mbox{Div}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{m}_{1}\\
\boldsymbol{m}_{2}\\
\boldsymbol{m}_{3}\end{array}\right)\right],\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\langle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\,\boldsymbol{n},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}x-\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\langle\mbox{Div}\left[\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{m}_{1}\\
\boldsymbol{m}_{2}\\
\boldsymbol{m}_{3}\end{array}\right)\right]\,\boldsymbol{n},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}x-\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\langle\mbox{Div}\left[\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{m}_{1}\,n_{1}\\
\boldsymbol{m}_{2}\,n_{2}\\
\boldsymbol{m}_{3}\,n_{3}\end{array}\right)\right]\cdot\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}},\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}x

\displaystyle+\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}\langle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\boldsymbol{m}_{1}\\
\boldsymbol{m}_{2}\\
\boldsymbol{m}_{3}\end{array}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{n},D^{n}\delta\boldsymbol{u}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\,\mbox{d}x\,.

where

\boldsymbol{m}_{i}=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)

+

a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)

+

\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)

and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}

+

\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}
.

We are now in a position to write the equilibrium equation and the boundary conditions without body forces and external load for the strain gradient model for the cylindrical plate bending since the only variations that remain are
\delta\boldsymbol{u}
and
D^{n}_{b}\delta\boldsymbol{u}
which are independent with respect each other:

\displaystyle\mbox{Div}\left[\widetilde{\sigma}-\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right)\right]=\boldsymbol{0}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\mbox{in}\,\Omega

\displaystyle\Big(2\mu_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{sym}\,\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}+\lambda_{\mbox{\tiny macro}}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)\,\boldsymbol{n}\hskip 31.2982pt

\displaystyle-\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}^{2}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\right)\,\boldsymbol{n}\hskip 31.2982pt

(231)

\displaystyle-\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big[\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)n_{i}\Big]\cdot\boldsymbol{D^{\tau}}=\boldsymbol{0}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\mbox{on}\,\partial\Omega

\displaystyle\left(\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(a_{1}\,\mbox{dev}\,\mbox{sym}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+a_{2}\,\mbox{skew}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)+\frac{2}{9}\,a_{3}\,\mbox{tr}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}\boldsymbol{\mbox{D}u}\right)\boldsymbol{\mathbbm{1}}\Big)n_{i}\right)\boldsymbol{n}=\boldsymbol{0}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\mbox{on}\,\partial\Omega

Appendix G
Relations of parameters in terms of the classical Mindlin-Eringen formulation

G.1
Reduced Mindlin-Eringen formulation in terms of the classical Mindlin-Eringen formulation

The curvature energy in eq.(
17
) can be represented in index form as

\displaystyle W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\Bigg[a_{1}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left(P_{jk}+P_{kj}\right)-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{jk}P_{mm}\Bigg)_{,i}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left(P_{jk}+P_{kj}\right)-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{jk}P_{mm}\Bigg)_{,i}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt+a_{2}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left(P_{jk}-P_{kj}\right)\Bigg)_{,i}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left(P_{jk}-P_{kj}\right)\Bigg)_{,i}+\frac{2}{9}a_{3}\delta_{jk}P_{mm,i}\delta_{jk}P_{nn,i}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\Bigg[a_{1}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left(P_{jk,i}+P_{kj,i}\right)-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{jk}P_{mm,i}\Bigg)\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left(P_{jk,i}+P_{kj,i}\right)-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{jk}P_{mm,i}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt+a_{2}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left(P_{jk,i}-P_{kj,i}\right)\Bigg)\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left(P_{jk,i}-P_{kj,i}\right)\Bigg)+\frac{2}{3}a_{3}P_{mm,i}P_{nn,i}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\Bigg[a_{1}\Bigg(\frac{1}{4}\left(P_{jk,i}+P_{kj,i}\right)\left(P_{jk,i}+P_{kj,i}\right)-\frac{1}{3}\left(P_{jk,i}+P_{kj,i}\right)\delta_{jk}P_{mm,i}+\frac{1}{9}\delta_{jk}P_{mm,i}\delta_{jk}P_{nn,i}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt+a_{2}\frac{1}{4}\Bigg(P_{jk,i}P_{jk,i}-2P_{jk,i}P_{kj,i}+P_{kj,i}P_{kj,i}\Bigg)+\frac{2}{3}a_{3}P_{mm,i}P_{nn,i}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\Bigg[a_{1}\Bigg(\frac{1}{4}\left(P_{jk,i}+P_{kj,i}\right)\left(P_{jk,i}+P_{kj,i}\right)-\frac{2}{3}P_{nn,i}P_{mm,i}+\frac{1}{3}P_{mm,i}P_{nn,i}\Bigg)

(232)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt+a_{2}\frac{1}{4}\Bigg(P_{jk,i}P_{jk,i}-2P_{jk,i}P_{kj,i}+P_{kj,i}P_{kj,i}\Bigg)+\frac{2}{3}a_{3}P_{mm,i}P_{nn,i}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\Bigg[\frac{a_{1}}{4}\left(P_{jk,i}P_{jk,i}+2P_{jk,i}P_{kj,i}+P_{kj,i}P_{kj,i}\right)-\frac{a_{1}}{3}P_{nn,i}P_{mm,i}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt+\frac{a_{2}}{2}\Bigg(P_{jk,i}P_{jk,i}-P_{jk,i}P_{kj,i}\Bigg)+\frac{2}{3}a_{3}P_{mm,i}P_{nn,i}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\Bigg[\frac{a_{1}}{2}\left(P_{jk,i}P_{jk,i}+P_{jk,i}P_{kj,i}\right)+\frac{a_{2}}{2}\Bigg(P_{jk,i}P_{jk,i}-P_{jk,i}P_{kj,i}\Bigg)+\frac{2a_{3}-a_{1}}{3}P_{mm,i}P_{nn,i}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\Bigg[\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}}{2}P_{jk,i}P_{jk,i}+\frac{a_{1}-a_{2}}{2}P_{jk,i}P_{kj,i}+\frac{2a_{3}-a_{1}}{3}P_{mm,i}P_{nn,i}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}}{4}\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{ijk}+\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\frac{a_{1}-a_{2}}{4}\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{ikj}+\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\frac{2a_{3}-a_{1}}{6}\chi_{imm}\chi_{inn}\,,

which, compared to eq.(
16
), gives the following relations between the curvature parameters

\displaystyle\widehat{a}_{1,2,3,5,8,11,14,15}

\displaystyle=0\,,\hskip 17.00024pt\widehat{a}_{4}=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\frac{2a_{3}-a_{1}}{3}\,,\hskip 17.00024pt\widehat{a}_{10}=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}}{2}\,,\hskip 17.00024pt\widehat{a}_{13}=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\frac{a_{1}-a_{2}}{2}\,.

(233)

G.2
Relaxed micromorphic formulation in terms of the classical Mindlin-Eringen formulation

Given the following expressions

\displaystyle\mbox{sym Curl}\boldsymbol{P}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\epsilon_{jnp}P_{in,p}+\epsilon_{inp}P_{jn,p}\right)e_{i}\otimes e_{j}\,,

\displaystyle\mbox{tr Curl}\boldsymbol{P}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{knp}P_{kn,p}\,,

(234)

\displaystyle\mbox{dev sym Curl}\boldsymbol{P}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\epsilon_{jnp}P_{in,p}+\epsilon_{inp}P_{jn,p}-\frac{2}{3}\epsilon_{knp}P_{kn,p}\delta_{ij}\right)e_{i}\otimes e_{j}\,,

\displaystyle\mbox{skew Curl}\boldsymbol{P}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\epsilon_{jnp}P_{in,p}-\epsilon_{inp}P_{jn,p}\right)e_{i}\otimes e_{j}\,,

it is possible to represent each single quadratic curvature term in eq.(
20
) in index form as

\displaystyle\rVert

\displaystyle\mbox{dev sym Curl}\boldsymbol{P}\lVert^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left(\epsilon_{jnp}P_{in,p}+\epsilon_{inp}P_{jn,p}-\frac{2}{3}\epsilon_{knp}P_{kn,p}\delta_{ij}\right)\left(\epsilon_{jmq}P_{im,q}+\epsilon_{imq}P_{jm,q}-\frac{2}{3}\epsilon_{qrs}P_{qr,s}\delta_{ij}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\epsilon_{jnp}P_{in,p}+\epsilon_{inp}P_{jn,p}-\frac{2}{3}\epsilon_{knp}P_{kn,p}\delta_{ij}\right)\epsilon_{jmq}P_{im,q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\epsilon_{jnp}P_{in,p}+\epsilon_{inp}P_{jn,p}\epsilon_{jmq}P_{im,q}-\frac{2}{3}\epsilon_{knp}P_{kn,p}\delta_{ij}\epsilon_{jmq}P_{im,q}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\delta_{mn}\delta_{pq}-\delta_{nq}\delta_{mp}\right)P_{in,p}P_{im,q}+\frac{1}{2}\begin{vmatrix}\delta_{ij}&\delta_{im}&\delta_{iq}\\
\delta_{nj}&\delta_{nm}&\delta_{nq}\\
\delta_{pj}&\delta_{pm}&\delta_{pq}\end{vmatrix}P_{jn,p}P_{im,q}-\frac{1}{3}\begin{vmatrix}\delta_{ki}&\delta_{km}&\delta_{kq}\\
\delta_{ni}&\delta_{nm}&\delta_{nq}\\
\delta_{pi}&\delta_{pm}&\delta_{pq}\end{vmatrix}P_{kn,p}P_{im,q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\left(P_{im,p}P_{im,p}-P_{in,m}P_{im,n}\right)

(235)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left(\delta_{ij}\delta_{nm}\delta_{pq}+\delta_{im}\delta_{nq}\delta_{pj}+\delta_{iq}\delta_{nj}\delta_{pm}-\delta_{pj}\delta_{nm}\delta_{iq}-\delta_{pm}\delta_{nq}\delta_{ij}-\delta_{pq}\delta_{nj}\delta_{im}\right)P_{jn,p}P_{im,q}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\left(\delta_{ki}\delta_{nm}\delta_{pq}+\delta_{km}\delta_{nq}\delta_{pi}+\delta_{kq}\delta_{ni}\delta_{pm}-\delta_{pi}\delta_{nm}\delta_{kq}-\delta_{pm}\delta_{nq}\delta_{ki}-\delta_{pq}\delta_{ni}\delta_{km}\right)P_{kn,p}P_{im,q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\left(P_{im,p}P_{im,p}-P_{in,m}P_{im,n}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left(P_{im,p}P_{im,p}+P_{jn,j}P_{ii,n}+P_{jj,m}P_{im,i}-P_{jm,j}P_{im,i}-P_{in,m}P_{im,n}-P_{jj,p}P_{ii,p}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\left(P_{im,p}P_{im,p}+P_{kn,i}P_{ik,n}+P_{ki,m}P_{im,k}-P_{km,i}P_{im,k}-P_{in,m}P_{im,n}-P_{ki,p}P_{ik,p}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{2}{3}P_{im,p}P_{im,p}-\frac{2}{3}P_{in,m}P_{im,n}+\frac{1}{3}P_{km,i}P_{im,k}+\frac{1}{3}P_{ki,p}P_{ik,p}-\frac{1}{3}P_{kn,i}P_{ik,n}-\frac{1}{3}P_{ki,m}P_{im,k}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}P_{jn,j}P_{ii,n}+\frac{1}{2}P_{jj,m}P_{im,i}-\frac{1}{2}P_{jm,j}P_{im,i}-\frac{1}{2}P_{jj,p}P_{ii,p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{2}{3}\,\chi_{ijk}\chi_{ijk}-\frac{2}{3}\,\chi_{ijk}\chi_{kji}+\frac{1}{3}\,\chi_{ijk}\chi_{jik}+\frac{1}{3}\,\chi_{ijk}\chi_{ikj}-\frac{1}{3}\,\chi_{ijk}\chi_{kij}-\frac{1}{3}\,\chi_{ijk}\chi_{jki}+\chi_{iik}\chi_{kjj}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\chi_{jjk}\chi_{iik}-\frac{1}{2}\,\chi_{ijj}\chi_{ikk}\,,

\displaystyle\rVert

\displaystyle\mbox{skew Curl}\boldsymbol{P}\lVert^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\left(\epsilon_{jnp}P_{in,p}-\epsilon_{inp}P_{jn,p}\right)\left(\epsilon_{jmq}P_{im,q}-\epsilon_{imq}P_{jm,q}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\epsilon_{jnp}P_{in,p}-\epsilon_{inp}P_{jn,p}\right)\epsilon_{jmq}P_{im,q}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\epsilon_{jnp}\epsilon_{jmq}P_{in,p}P_{im,q}-\epsilon_{inp}\epsilon_{jmq}P_{jn,p}P_{im,q}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\delta_{mn}\delta_{pq}-\delta_{nq}\delta_{mp}\right)P_{in,p}P_{im,q}-\frac{1}{2}\begin{vmatrix}\delta_{ij}&\delta_{im}&\delta_{iq}\\
\delta_{nj}&\delta_{nm}&\delta_{nq}\\
\delta_{pj}&\delta_{pm}&\delta_{pq}\end{vmatrix}P_{jn,p}P_{im,q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\delta_{mn}\delta_{pq}-\delta_{nq}\delta_{mp}\right)P_{in,p}P_{im,q}

(236)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left(\delta_{ij}\delta_{nm}\delta_{pq}+\delta_{im}\delta_{nq}\delta_{pj}+\delta_{iq}\delta_{nj}\delta_{pm}-\delta_{pj}\delta_{nm}\delta_{iq}-\delta_{pm}\delta_{nq}\delta_{ij}-\delta_{pq}\delta_{nj}\delta_{im}\right)P_{jn,p}P_{im,q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(P_{im,p}P_{im,p}-P_{in,m}P_{im,n}-P_{im,p}P_{im,p}-P_{jn,j}P_{ii,n}-P_{jj,m}P_{im,i}+P_{jm,j}P_{im,i}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\left.+P_{in,m}P_{im,n}+P_{jj,p}P_{ii,p}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{ijk}-\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{kji}-\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{ijk}+\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{kji}-\chi_{iik}\,\chi_{kjj}-\chi_{iik}\,\chi_{kjj}+\chi_{iik}\,\chi_{jjk}+\chi_{ijj}\,\chi_{ikk}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(-2\chi_{iik}\,\chi_{kjj}+\chi_{iik}\,\chi_{jjk}+\chi_{ijj}\,\chi_{ikk}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\mbox{tr}^{2}\mbox{ Curl}\boldsymbol{P}=

\displaystyle\,\epsilon_{inp}P_{in,p}\epsilon_{jmq}P_{jm,q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\delta_{ij}\delta_{nm}\delta_{pq}+\delta_{im}\delta_{nq}\delta_{pj}+\delta_{iq}\delta_{nj}\delta_{pm}-\delta_{pj}\delta_{nm}\delta_{iq}-\delta_{pm}\delta_{nq}\delta_{ij}-\delta_{pq}\delta_{nj}\delta_{im}\right)P_{in,p}P_{jm,q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{im,p}P_{im,p}+P_{in,j}P_{ji,n}+P_{ij,m}P_{jm,i}-P_{im,j}P_{jm,i}-P_{in,m}P_{im,n}-P_{ij,p}P_{ji,p}

(237)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{ijk}+\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{jki}+\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{kij}-\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{jik}-\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{kji}-\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{ikj}\,.

Inserting all the expressions written before in eq.(
20
) we have

\displaystyle W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}a_{1}\Bigg(\frac{2}{3}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{ijk}-\frac{2}{3}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{kji}+\frac{1}{3}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{jik}+\frac{1}{3}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{ikj}

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt-\frac{1}{3}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{kij}-\frac{1}{3}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{jki}+\chi_{iik}\chi_{kjj}-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{jjk}\chi_{iik}-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{ijj}\chi_{ikk}\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}a_{2}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left(-2\chi_{iik}\chi_{kjj}+\chi_{iik}\chi_{jjk}+\chi_{ijj}\chi_{ikk}\right)\Bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{6}a_{3}\Bigg(\chi_{ijk}\chi_{ijk}+\chi_{ijk}\chi_{jki}+\chi_{ijk}\chi_{kij}-\chi_{ijk}\chi_{jik}-\chi_{ijk}\chi_{kji}-\chi_{ijk}\chi_{ikj}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{1}}{3}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{ijk}-\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{1}}{3}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{kji}+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{1}}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{jik}+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{1}}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{ikj}

(238)

\displaystyle-\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{1}}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{kij}-\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{1}}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{jki}+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{1}}{2}\chi_{iik}\chi_{kjj}-\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{1}}{4}\chi_{jjk}\chi_{iik}

\displaystyle-\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{1}}{4}\chi_{ijj}\chi_{ikk}-\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{2}}{2}\chi_{iik}\chi_{kjj}+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{2}}{4}\chi_{iik}\chi_{jjk}+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{2}}{4}\chi_{ijj}\chi_{ikk}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{3}}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{ijk}+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{3}}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{jki}+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{3}}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{kij}-\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{3}}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{jik}

\displaystyle-\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{3}}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{kji}-\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,a_{3}}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{ikj}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\left(2a_{1}+a_{3}\right)}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{ijk}-\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\left(2a_{1}+a_{3}\right)}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{kji}+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\left(a_{1}-a_{3}\right)}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{jik}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\left(a_{1}-a_{3}\right)}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{ikj}+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\left(a_{3}-a_{1}\right)}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{kij}+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\left(a_{3}-a_{1}\right)}{6}\chi_{ijk}\chi_{jki}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\left(2a_{1}-a_{2}\right)}{4}\chi_{iik}\chi_{kjj}+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\left(a_{2}-a_{1}\right)}{4}\chi_{iik}\chi_{jjk}+\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\left(a_{2}-a_{1}\right)}{4}\chi_{ijj}\chi_{ikk}

which compared to eq.(
16
) gives the following relations between the curvature parameters

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\widehat{a}_{1}

\displaystyle=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\frac{2a_{1}-a_{2}}{4}\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\widehat{a}_{2}=\widehat{a}_{5}=\widehat{a}_{8}=0\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\widehat{a}_{3}=\widehat{a}_{4}=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\frac{a_{2}-a_{1}}{2}\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\widehat{a}_{10}=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\frac{2a_{1}+a_{3}}{3}\,,

(239)

\displaystyle\widehat{a}_{11}

\displaystyle=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\frac{a_{3}-a_{1}}{6}\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\widehat{a}_{13}=\widehat{a}_{14}=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\frac{a_{1}-a_{3}}{3}\,,\hskip 8.50012pt\widehat{a}_{15}=-\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\,\frac{2a_{1}+a_{3}}{3}\,.

The most simple curvature
\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\rVert\mbox{Curl}\boldsymbol{P}\lVert^{2}
corresponds to
a_{1}=a_{2}=a_{3}=1
and is therefore given by
\widehat{a}_{2,3,4,5,8,11,13,14}=0
,
\widehat{a}_{1}=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}}{4}
,
\widehat{a}_{10}=-\widehat{a}_{15}=\mu\,L_{c}
.

G.3
Second Gradient formulation in terms of the classical Mindlin-Eringen formulation

Given that
\chi_{ijk}:=u_{k,ij}=\chi_{jik}
, the curvature energy in eq.(
133
) can be represented in index form as

\displaystyle W_{\tiny\mbox{curv}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\Bigg[a_{1}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left(u_{i,jk}+u_{j,ik}\right)-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{ij}u_{m,mk}\Bigg)u_{i,jk}+\frac{1}{2}a_{2}\left(u_{i,jk}-u_{j,ik}\right)u_{i,jk}+\frac{2}{9}a_{3}\,u_{m,mk}\,u_{i,ik}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\Bigg[a_{1}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left(\chi_{jki}+\chi_{ikj}\right)-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{ij}\chi_{kmm}\Bigg)\chi_{jki}+\frac{1}{2}a_{2}\left(\chi_{jki}-\chi_{ikj}\right)\chi_{jki}+\frac{2}{9}a_{3}\,\chi_{kmm}\,\chi_{kii}\Bigg]

(240)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\Bigg[\frac{1}{2}a_{1}\,\chi_{jki}\,\chi_{jki}+\frac{1}{2}a_{1}\,\chi_{ikj}\,\chi_{jki}-\frac{1}{3}a_{1}\,\chi_{kmm}\,\chi_{jki}+\frac{1}{3}a_{2}\,\chi_{jki}\,\chi_{jki}+\frac{1}{3}a_{2}\,\chi_{ikj}\,\chi_{jki}+\frac{2}{9}a_{3}\,\chi_{kmm}\,\chi_{kii}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\Bigg[\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}}{2}\,\chi_{jki}\,\chi_{jki}+\frac{a_{1}-a_{2}}{2}\,\chi_{ikj}\,\chi_{jki}-\frac{1}{3}a_{1}\,\chi_{kmm}\,\chi_{jki}+\frac{2}{9}a_{3}\,\chi_{kmm}\,\chi_{kii}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu\,L_{c}^{2}}{2}\Bigg[\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}}{2}\,\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{ijk}+\frac{a_{1}-a_{2}}{2}\,\chi_{ijk}\,\chi_{kji}+\frac{2a_{3}-3a_{1}}{9}a_{3}\,\chi_{ijj}\,\chi_{ikk}\Bigg]\,,

which compared to eq.(
132
) gives the following relations between the curvature parameters

\displaystyle\widehat{a}_{1,3}

\displaystyle=0\,,\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\widehat{a}_{2}=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\frac{2a_{3}-3a_{1}}{18}\,,\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\widehat{a}_{4}=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}}{4}\,,\hskip 17.00024pt\hskip 17.00024pt\widehat{a}_{5}=\mu\,L_{c}^{2}\frac{a_{1}-a_{2}}{4}\,.

(241)
